Convolution via FFT:
```
def full_conv(a):
    n=a.size
    L=next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    fa=rfft(a,L)
    c=irfft(fa*fa,L, n=2*n-1)
    return np.maximum(c,0) # due numerical negative? But q for nonnegative should nonneg but numerical small negative; for max use max(c,0)
```

Let's detail implement high-quality optimizer.

### Exact score and gradient
- Normalize p to sum 1. Score = 2*n*max(full_conv(p)). For exact final use evaluate_sequence (which does not normalize; but with sum1 same). It clips to 1000.
- For internal approximations use LSE.

### LSE optimization with softmax θ
Let's implement robust L-BFGS-B:
```
def lse_logsumexp(q, beta):
    m=q.max()
    z=beta*(q-m)
    e=np.exp(z)
    return m + np.log(e.sum())/beta
```
Gradient wrt θ:
- w=softmax(β(q-qmax)), sum1.
- h=2*valid_conv(w,p)
- dot=p·h
- grad=p*(h-dot)
But h magnitude? If q ~1/n, valid_conv sums w r p_r, magnitude ~sum p * p ~1/n? times 2. h ~ O(1/n). grad ~p*h ~1/n^2. Gradient tiny. L-BFGS step maybe? Could scale objective by n to improve: Objective score-like = 2n LSE? If we minimize n*LSE or 2n LSE, gradient scaled by n. The LSE objective difference O(1/n), so multiply by n for stable. But β should scale with n: β = λ n, λ maybe 10-1000. Then β q ~ O(λ). h ~O(1/n). grad sums O(1/n^2); scaling by n gives O(1/n). Use objective `n * LSE` or `2n * LSE`. Let's define F = n * LSE(β q) maybe. Then score approx F*? Since LSE≈max q, n*LSE≈n max = score/2. Multiply by 2 to score scale: F=2n LSE. Gradient scaled by 2n: 2n*∂LSE = 2n*β? Actually gradient: ∂LSE/∂p = ∑w_k C_k = valid_conv(w,p) (no β? Wait ∂LSE/∂p = β ∑w C? Let's recalc: F_lse= (1/β) log∑e^{βq}. Derivative = ∑ w_k C_k (not β!). Because derivative (1/β)*β w_i C_i = w_i C_i. Ah previous fun=βM+log sum = β LSE? They used fun=βM+log sum = β*(1/β logsum?) = logsum? Wait if F=βM+log∑e^{β(c-M)} = log∑e^{βc}, not (1/β)LSE. They optimized log-sum-exp, not normalized LSE. Its derivative includes β. Objective scale ~β log k. That's okay. But for stable, use normalized LSE: `(1/beta) logsumexp(beta q)` derivative =∑w C. Then multiply by 2n to get score scale; gradient =2n valid_conv(w,p). This avoids β in gradient. That may be better. Let's use F = 2*n*(max q + log(sum exp(β(q-max)))/β). Gradient wrt p = 2*n*valid_conv(w,p). Then wrt θ = p*(G-dot). This objective approaches exact score as β→∞. Nice.

So:
```
def obj_grad(theta):
    p=softmax(theta)
    q=full_conv(p)
    m=q.max()
    z=beta*(q-m); w=exp(z); w/=sum
    G=2*n*valid_conv(w,p)  # units score
    Gp=G  # derivative wrt p on simplex (including scale)
    grad=p*(G - p·G)
    val=2*n*(m + log(mean? actually log(sum exp z)/β)) # If sum not mean. logsum = m + log(s)/β.
    return val,grad
```
This is smooth. For β high, w active.

Alternative: Use direct **top-K active average** objective F=2n*(mean of top K q). Gradient =2n/K*valid_conv(w,p). Can optimize with L-BFGS; topK non-differentiable but choose K small (e.g., 10). This may approximate max better than LSE for same β. But LSE smooth enough.

Parameterization: θ length n-1 to avoid shift. Map to p:
```
def softmax_y(y):
   # y length n-1, set theta_n-1=0? p_i=exp(y_i) for i<n-1, p_last=exp(0)=1? This breaks symmetry; better include all θ but fix last=0.

    L=2*n-1
    qv=zeros(L); qv[2*i]=1

We'll run a loop:
- q=full_conv(p), M=q.max()
- active weights w: top K? Maybe average top K (K=ceil(0.1*n) or adaptive). Compute G=valid_conv(w,p).

 parent = normalize(height_sequence_1) if available else ones(256)
 best = parent, exact score
 rng = default_rng(seed)

 lengths = [256,512,1024,2048] maybe depending budget.
 For each L in lengths:
   allocate time based on length and remaining.
   inits = [interp(parent,L), interp(best,L), reversed, random...]
   For best few inits:
      p = lse_refine(p, L, beta_list, stop)
      p = coordinate_refine(p, L, stop)
      try lp_update if L<=1024
      track best exact
   # if time enough, random restarts at L

```

```
def _line_search_away(qp,p,i,n,N):
   N=n-1
   k=all_k  # global
   C = counts_full[n] (global)
   valid = (k>=i)&(k<=i+n-1)
   c = C.copy(); c[valid]-=2; c[2*i]+=1
   qv = c / (N*N)
   # window sum
   cum = precomputed? p changes, compute cum = np.concatenate(([0], np.cumsum(p)))
   low = np.maximum(0, k-(n-1)); high = np.minimum(n-1,k)
   win = cum[high+1] - cum[low]
   exclude = p[k-i] where valid else 0
   qpv = (win - exclude)/N
   line search quadratic
```

### Numerical stability
- `full_conv` with FFT may produce negative tiny; for exact max, negative shouldn't affect (max positive). But for coordinate line search, base q might have tiny negative; okay. We can `c = np.maximum(c,0)`? If we zero tiny negatives, sum q maybe slightly off (should 1). For objective max, zeroing negatives harmless. Method `_fwh_init(n, shape='flat', rng)`:
- Choose target q length L=2n-1 nonnegative sum1 with low max. Candidate shapes:

- Compute P via iterative:
   `p = ones(n)/n`
   for iters 5:
      P = fft(p, n=next_fast_len(2n))
      Q = fft(q_target, n=same)
      # choose sqrt phase: P_new_fft = P * sqrt(Q/(P*P? no))
      Simpler: P = sqrt(Q) principal, p=irfft(P, n)[:n]; p=clip(p,0,None); normalize.
   But principal sqrt may yield p with support both pos/neg and zeros. Maybe after several? We'll just clip.
- Could use `p = np.real_if_close(np.fft.irfft(np.sqrt(np.fft.fft(q_target, L)), L))[:n]`. Since q_target positive, DFT may be complex; sqrt gives branch. The resulting finite sequence may have negative entries; clip. Normalize. We can try multiple target shapes and random phase twists: multiply Q by exp(2iφ) before sqrt? The square root sign can be chosen to get different polynomial degree. For polynomial square root, we need a spectral factor. On unit circle, P(z)^2=Q(z). Principal sqrt may pick branch where p has support both positive and negative (degree up to 2n). To enforce degree n, we can choose phase such that linear phase? Suppose Q(z)=P(z)^2 has degree 2n-2. If P has degree n-1, Q has linear phase e^{-iθ(n-1)} if p positive? The DFT of q_target centered? We can center q by shifting? Let's not overcomplicate. This heuristic may produce candidates if clipping yields good.

### Let's design code structure in detail
Imports: numpy as np, time, math, scipy.fft (rfft, irfft, next_fast_len), scipy.optimize (minimize, linprog). Could use `scipy.linalg`? no.
Global caches: `_FFT_LEN = {}`, `_COUNTS = {}`.

Functions:
- `_fft_len(m)`.
- `_full_conv(a)`: length n. If n=0. L=next_fast_len(2n-1). fa=rfft(a,L); c=irfft(fa*fa,L, n=2n-1)?? `irfft` with n parameter? SciPy `irfft(a, n=None, norm=None)`. To specify output length, use `irfft(fa*fa, n=L)`. Slice. Or np.fft.irfft. Use `scipy.fft.irfft(fa*fa, n=L)`. Clip 0. Return.
Potential numerical: `fa*fa` real? fa complex; product complex with tiny imag? It should be real if a real? fa*fa not conjugate, can be complex. inverse gives real? Since product of DFT with itself, coefficients of square are real but FFT values not conjugate symmetric? The inverse of product is real if sequence real? Yes square of real sequence (linear convolution) is real; due rounding small imag. `irfft` expects Hermitian; `fa*fa` may not be exactly Hermitian? For real a, DFT X[k]; product X[k]^2. Its inverse is real because X[-k]=conj X[k], product X[-k]^2=conj(X[k])^2 = conj(X[k]^2), so Hermitian. Good.
- `_cross_conv(a,b)`: for f*g full length n1+n2-1. Use L=next_fast_len(m); rffts; irfft. Clip 0.

```
def _moments(p, w, L, n):
   ra = rfft(p, L)
   q = irfft(ra*ra, L)[:2*n-1]
   if w is None: return q, None
   rap = rfft(p[::-1], L)
   rw = rfft(w, L)
   out = irfft(rw*rap, L)[n-1:2*n-1]
   return q, out
```
But in objective we need q first to compute w. We compute q, then w, then valid. That's 2 rffts p (ra, rap) +1 rfft w +2 irffts. Good. Could compute rap before? yes.

```
def _grad_active_direct(active, p):
   n=p.size; G=zeros(n)
   for k in active:
      lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
      if lo>hi: continue
      # r = k - arange(lo,hi+1)
      r = k - np.arange(lo,hi+1)
      G[lo:hi+1] += p[r]
   # If using average over K, divide by len(active). Factor 2 or 4n irrelevant for argmin/max.
   return G / len(active)
```
This is O(K*n). For K up to 16 and n4096, 65k per iteration, okay. But active set update after each coordinate move; sorting O(n log n). We can use topK by `np.argpartition(q, -K)[-K:]`. For n=4096, 8191 values, okay.

   a = np.array([-4,4]) choose?

   fa=rfft(a, L)
   c=irfft(fa*fa, L, n=m?)

Use all n variables:

def _p_from_y(y):
   z=y - y.max()
   z=np.clip(z, -50, 0) # Wait if max subtract, z<=0; lower clip -50.
   e=np.exp(z)
   p=e/e.sum()
   return p
def _y_from_p(p):
   # p positive; p+eps
   y=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
   y -= y.max()
   return y
```
If we clip z, p_from_y not exactly softmax(y) for z<-50; okay. Initial y max subtract. Softmax with max subtract. Good.

But if we clip at -50, p min ~1.9e-22; okay. If optimal needs zero, fine.

L-BFGS function:
```
def _make_lse_fn(n, beta):
   L=_fft_len(2*n-1); m=2*n-1
   def f(y):
      p=_p_from_y(y)
      fa = rfft(p,L)
      q=irfft(fa*fa,L)[:m]; q=np.maximum(q,0)
      mq=q.max()
      z=beta*(q-mq)
      z=np.clip(z,-50,0)
      e=np.exp(z); s=e.sum(); w=e/s
      rap = rfft(p[::-1], L)
      rw = rfft(w, L)
      vc = irfft(rw*rap, L)
      G = 4.0*n*vc[n-1:2*n-1]
      gdot = float(p @ G)
      grad = p*(G - gdot)
      val = 2.0*n*(mq + math.log(s)/beta)
      if not finite: return 1e100, zeros
      return val,grad
   return f
```
Need `math.log(s)` if s>0. z max 0, at active if q=m. s≥1? If q has max entries count>0, exp(0) count at least1, s≥1. Good.

But if beta very large, z for near max maybe < -50 clipped to 0? No, z negative; if less than -50 exp underflow zero, okay. If all z clipped -50? But max entries z=0, exp1. Good.

Potential issue: `_p_from_y` with z max 0, if y contains nan/inf. Check.

for lam in lambdas:

   if beta<1: beta=1
   # maxfun based on remaining and n
   maxfun = ...
   res=minimize(make_fn, y0, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter':300,'maxfun':maxfun,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-9,'maxls':20})
   p_new = _p_from_y(res.x)
   sc = _score_internal(p_new)
   if finite and sc < best_sc: p = p_new; best_sc=sc else? maybe set y0=y0? 
   # For next beta, start from p_new (even if exact slightly worse) or p? Use p_new to follow LSE.
```
If res failed but finite x, use. If y_new nan, break.

### Additional technique: **Hair-cut active constraint line search**
Coordinate line search to u_i/e_i may be slow to reduce max by more than few percent. L-BFGS top active may be better. We can implement a **full natural gradient step with exact line search** using top-K active weights. Given active weights w, G=valid_conv(w,p). The natural gradient direction in log p is H = G - p·G (or G - mean). Update p_t=normalize(p * exp(-t H))? Since log p change -t H. This is a multiplicative step. The objective segment is not quadratic (nonlinear in p), but we can line search exact by evaluating full conv for candidate t using FFT. We can try several t (1/n, etc). This may be more global than coordinate. Let's implement? It can improve all coordinates based on active gradient. Cost: each candidate p_t, full conv FFT. For n=1024, 10 candidates*10 iterations =100 FFT, fine. Could be useful.

Algorithm `_natural_descent(p, stop)`:
- q=full_conv, M.
- active topK, w. G=valid_conv(w,p) (FFT or direct). H=G - p@G (for log gradient; should be zero sum? Sum H = sum G - sum pG? Not necessarily? G not weighted by p. For softmax natural gradient, h=G - p·G ensures dot(p,h)=0, sum h not zero. Multiplicative update p*exp(-η h) changes sum, normalize. H maybe large. Use η scale = 1/(max|H|) * step.
- For t in e.g. [1e-4,1e-3,0.005,0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3]: p_new = p * exp(-eta*t*H); normalize; score. If improve update. Iterate.
This is similar to mirror descent. It may be faster than L-BFGS? Maybe. Include as optional if time.

But coordinate/LSE enough. To keep code size manageable, maybe skip.

#### `_normalize`
```
def _normalize(a, fallback=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size==0: a=np.ones(fallback or 256)/...
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0, posinf=0, neginf=0)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if not finite or s<1e-12: a=np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    return a/s
```

def _optimize_length(L, stop, rng, parent_arr, best_global):
   if time.monotonic() > stop-5: return
   inits=[]
   inits.append(_interp(parent_arr,L))
   if best_global is not None and best_global.size != L: inits.append(_interp(best_global,L))
   inits.append(_normalize(np.interp(x0? parent reversed))) # reverse interp

   if L <= 1024: inits.append(_random_smooth(L,rng))

       if time... break

       if finite: scored.append((sc,a))
   scored.sort()
   for sc0,a in scored[:2]:

       p=a
       # phase coordinate 35%

```

### `coordinate_refine` implementation details
Pseudo:
```
def _coordinate_refine(a0, stop, seed=0, maxmoves=None):
    p=_normalize(a0); n=p.size
    q=_full_conv(p); M=float(q.max())
    best=p.copy(); bestM=M; bestS=2*n*M
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    if maxmoves is None: maxmoves = 4000 if n<=256 else 2000 if n<=1024 else 800
    k_all = _get_k(L)
    C_all = _get_counts(n)
    no_imp=0
    moves=0
    while time.monotonic() < stop-0.2 and moves < maxmoves:
        moves += 1
        # if q not consistent, recompute occasionally
        if moves % 25 ==0:
            q=_full_conv(p); M=q.max()
        # active
        K = max(3, min(24, max(1, n//64)))
        if 2*n-1 <= K: active = np.arange(2*n-1)
        else:
           active = np.argpartition(q, -K)[-K:]
        # direct G
        G = np.zeros(n)
        for kk in active:
            lo=max(0,kk-n+1); hi=min(n-1,kk)
            if lo<=hi:
                js = np.arange(lo, hi+1)
                G[js] += p[kk-js]
        G /= len(active)
        # candidate directions
        low = int(np.argmin(G)); high=int(np.argmax(G))
        cands=[]
        cands.append((high, 'away'))
        cands.append((low, 'toward'))
        # random candidates
        if n>2 and rng.random()<0.8:
             r1=int(rng.integers(n)); r2=int(rng.integers(n))
             # decide direction based on G relative
             cands.append((r1, 'away' if G[r1] >= np.median(G) else 'toward'))
             cands.append((r2, 'away' if G[r2] >= np.median(G) else 'toward'))
        improved=False
        for i,dir in cands:
            if time.monotonic() > stop-0.2: break
            if dir=='away': t,Mn,qn = _line_search_away(q,p,i,n,k_all,C_all)
            else: t,Mn,qn = _line_search_vertex(q,p,i,n,k_all,C_all)
            if t is not None and Mn < M - 1e-12:
                 if dir=='away': p_new=(1-t)*p; N=n-1; if N>0: p_new += t*(1/N)*(1 - e_i?) etc
                 else: p_new=(1-t)*p; p_new[i]+=t
                 p_new/=sum
                 p=p_new; q=qn; M=Mn
                 if M < bestM - 1e-12: bestM=M; best=p.copy(); bestS=2*n*M
                 improved=True
                 break # one update per outer, recompute G
        if not improved:
            no_imp +=1
            if no_imp >= 8:
                # perturb
                scale = 0.05 * n / n? mean=1/n. Use sigma=0.05/n? relative 5%
                sig=0.02 * (1.0/n) * min(5,no_imp) ?
                p = p + rng.normal(0, sig, n)
                p=np.clip(p,0,None); s=p.sum(); if s<1e-12: p=best.copy()
                else p/=s
                q=_full_conv(p); M=q.max()
                no_imp=0
                if M < bestM: update
                if no? limit perturbations maybe if time
            # if many perturb no improve, break
            if no_imp > 60: break
        else:
            no_imp=0
    if bestM < M: p=best.copy(); q? not needed
    return p, bestS
```
Need avoid median G each iteration (sort). We can compare G[r] > G[q?]. Use `if G[r] >= G[low] + (G[high]-G[low])*0.7` high? Simpler: random direction away. Or skip random directions to save. Use high away, low toward plus one random away and one random toward? Let's do 4 candidates: `(high,'away'), (low,'toward'), (r1,'away'), (r2,'toward')`. No median.

Line search functions return None if no improvement > `tol`.
Use global `_T_COARSE` and `_T_FINE`. But for large n, T perhaps too big. Use cached arrays.

```
def _line_search_quad(base, b, c, currentM):
    L=base.size
    t=_T_COARSE
    vals = base[None,:] + t[:,None]*b[None,:] + (t[:,None]*t[:,None])*c[None,:]
    maxs = vals.max(axis=1)
    # enforce nonnegative? not needed for max
    ib=int(np.argmin(maxs))
    if ib == 0 or maxs[ib] >= currentM - 1e-12: return None
    lo = t[ib-1] if ib>0 else 0.0
    hi = t[ib+1] if ib < len(t)-1 else 1.0
    if hi <= lo: hi = min(1.0, lo + 0.01)
    t2=np.linspace(lo,hi,48)
    vals2=...
    maxs2=...
    ib2=int(np.argmin(maxs2))
    if maxs2[ib2] >= currentM - 1e-12: return None
    tt=float(t2[ib2])
    qnew = base + tt*b + tt*tt*c
    qnew = np.maximum(qnew,0)
    return tt, float(maxs2[ib2]), qnew
```
If coarse best at 0 but refine near 0? If maxs[1] maybe slightly lower? ib not 0. Good. If ib>0 but lo=0 and hi small; refine includes 0; if best at 0 due to coarse grid, refine may find positive. Good.

For currentM, use `base.max()` maybe M. Since base clipped nonneg. Use `currentM = float(base.max())`.

T_coarse: `np.unique(np.concatenate(([0.0], np.geomspace(1e-5,1.0,40))))`. This includes non-uniform. But if optimum near 0.5, geoms covers. Good. For refine, linear lo-hi.

Memory allocation `vals` each line search. Could be okay. To reduce for many calls, we can choose T=32 for n>1024. Use global `_T_COARSE_SMALL` and `_T_COARSE_LARGE`. Or dynamic.

def _get_t_grid(L): return _T_COARSE if L<=4096 else _T_COARSE_SMALL
```
For n=4096 L=8191, T=32.

Line search vertex:
```
def _line_search_vertex(q,p,i,n,cov?):
    m=2*n-1
    qpv=np.zeros(m)
    qpv[i:i+n]=p
    base=q
    b=2.0*(qpv-base)
    c=base - 2.0*qpv
    c[2*i] += 1.0
    return _line_search_quad(base,b,c,float(M))
```
But qpv assigned p, b modifies? `b=2*(qpv-base)` new. c uses base. Good.

Line search away:
```
def _line_search_away(q,p,i,n,k_all,C_all):
   m=2*n-1
   N=n-1
   if N<=0: return None
   qv = C_all.astype(float).copy()
   if n>1:
      qv[i:i+n] -= 2.0
      qv[2*i] += 1.0
      N2=float(N*N); qv /= N2
   else ...
   cum=np.empty(n+1); cum[0]=0; cum[1:]=np.cumsum(p)
   low=np.maximum(0,k_all-(n-1))
   high=np.minimum(n-1,k_all)
   win=cum[high+1]-cum[low]
   valid = (k_all>=i)&(k_all<=i+n-1)
   exclude = np.where(valid, p[valid - i]? Need p[k_all-i] where valid. Can't index p with boolean of length m and shift? Do:
   idx = k_all[valid] - i
   exclude = zeros(m); exclude[valid]=p[idx]
   qpv=(win-exclude)/N
   b=2*(qpv - q); c=q -2*qpv + qv
   return _line_search_quad(q,b,c,M)
```
Need `k_all` and `C_all` precomputed for n; C_all float? Could store float. `C_all = _get_counts(n).astype(float)`.
`low`/`high` precomputable for n: `_get_bounds(n)` returns low, high, valid offsets? For each n. To save, cache `_K_INFO[n]=(k_all,C_float,low,high)`.
```
def _kinfo(n):
   m=2*n-1; k=np.arange(m); C=np.minimum(k+1,2*n-1-k).astype(float)
   low=np.maximum(0,k-(n-1)); high=np.minimum(n-1,k)
   return k,C,low,high
```
For exclude, `valid = (k>=i)&(k<=i+n-1)`. This is slice `i:i+n` true. We can avoid boolean:
```
exclude = np.zeros(m)
start=i; end=min(m,i+n)
exclude[start:end] = p[:end-start]  # because k-i from 0 to n-1
```
This is faster. Good. Also qv modification slice.
```
qv = C.copy()
qv[i:i+n] -=2
qv[2*i] +=1
qv /= N2
exclude=zeros(m); exclude[i:i+n]=p
```
Wait exclude should be p[k-i] for k i..i+n-1: p[0..n-1]. Yes. Good.
`qpv=(win-exclude)/N`.
For vertex qpv shift: `qpv[i:i+n]=p`.

#### Direct active G using active indices
Could optimize direct G with slice:
```
G = zeros(n)
for kk in active:
    lo=max(0, kk-n+1); hi=min(n-1, kk)
    if lo<=hi:
        # r = kk - lo ... kk-hi decreasing
        G[lo:hi+1] += p[kk-hi:kk-lo+1][::-1]? Simpler:
        r = kk - np.arange(lo,hi+1)
        G[lo:hi+1] += p[r]
```
This allocates r each active. K small. Could use slice reverse: `p[kk-hi : kk-lo+1]` gives r increasing? Example lo=0,hi=2, kk=3 -> r=3,2,1. slice p[1:4] = [p1,p2,p3], need [p3,p2,p1]. Use `p[kk-hi:kk-lo+1][::-1]`. That works if step reverse. But if kk-hi maybe negative? lo ensures kk-lo≤n-1, kk-hi≥0. Let's implement robust:
```
lo = max(0, kk-n+1); hi = min(n-1, kk)
length = hi-lo+1
G[lo:hi+1] += p[kk-hi:kk-lo+1][::-1]
```
Check kk=3,n=5: lo=0,hi=3 length4, kk-hi=0, kk-lo+1=4 -> p[0:4][::-1]=[p3,p2,p1,p0], corresponds r=3,2,1,0 for j=0..3. Good. kk=1: lo=0,hi=1, slice p[0:2][::-1]=[p1,p0]. def _lse_refine(a0, stop, lambdas=(10,50,200), maxfun_table=None):
    p=_normalize(a0); n=p.size
    best=p.copy(); best_sc=_score_internal(p)
    if time.monotonic()>stop or n<2: return best,best_sc
    qmax = _full_conv(p).max()
    # lambdas maybe adapt to n
    est = ...
    for lam in lambdas:
       if time.monotonic() > stop-2: break
       beta = lam / max(qmax,1e-300)
       if beta < 1: beta=1
       # cap beta maybe 1e7
       beta = min(beta, 1e7)
       # maxfun
       maxfun = _choose_maxfun(n, stop, est)
       if maxfun < 30: break
       try:
          fn = _make_lse_fn(n,beta,L)
          y0 = _y_from_p(p)
          res = minimize(fn, y0, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', options={...})
       except Exception: break
       if res.x is not None:
           p_new = _p_from_y(res.x)
           sc_new = _score_internal(p_new)
           if finite:
               # update p for next beta even if exact worse? Use if sc_new < best_sc or res.success
               if sc_new < best_sc:
                   best=p_new.copy(); best_sc=sc_new; p=best
               else:
                   # use p_new as start if not much worse? Could follow LSE; but may drift. Use if res.success and sc_new < best_sc + 0.005
                   if getattr(res,'success',False) and sc_new < best_sc + 0.005: p=p_new
               qmax = max(_full_conv(p).max(),1e-300) # for next beta; but if p unchanged, recompute maybe
           else: break
       else: break
       # update exact if best improved
    return best,best_sc
```

### `_random_smooth`
Let's implement a few styles with `rng`.
```
def _random_smooth(n, rng):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    choice=rng.integers(0,6)
    if choice==0:
       a=rng.uniform(-3,3); b=rng.uniform(-3,3)
       f=np.exp(a*x+b*(1-x)+rng.normal(0,0.3))
    elif choice==1:
       a=rng.uniform(0.2,8); b=rng.uniform(0.2,8)
       f=x**(a-1)*(1-x)**(b-1)
    elif choice==2:
       f=np.zeros(n)
       for _ in range(rng.integers(1,4)):
          c=rng.uniform(0,1); w=rng.uniform(0.03,0.25); amp=rng.uniform(0.5,2)
          f += amp*np.exp(-0.5*((x-c)/w)**2)
    else:
       f=np.ones(n)
       for _ in range(rng.integers(1,5)):
          m=int(rng.integers(1,9)); amp=rng.uniform(0.05,0.55); ph=rng.uniform(0,2*pi)
          f += amp*np.cos(np.pi*m*x+ph) # includes nonperiodic if m? endpoint values can differ for odd m.
    f=np.nan_to_num(f, nan=0, posinf=0, neginf=0)
    f=np.clip(f,0,None)
    return _normalize(f)
```
Could add small constant to ensure positive: `f = f + 0.01`? If clip zeros okay. For nonneg, zero allowed. Add `1e-6`.

- Compute P via iterative:
```
q = normalize(q)
L = next_fast_len(2*n) # maybe
p = ones(n)/n
for _ in range(3):
   P = rfft(p, L) # real positive
   Q = rfft(q, L)
   # We want P^2≈Q. Update P = P / sqrt(P^2/Q)? P_new = P * sqrt(Q/(P*P)) with branch.
   ratio = Q / (P*P)
   # sqrt complex; p_new = irfft(P * sqrt(ratio), n=L)[:n]
   ratio = np.where(Q==0,0,ratio)
   Pnew = P * np.sqrt(ratio)
   pn = irfft(Pnew, n=L)[:n]
   pn = np.maximum(pn,0); pn=pn/pn.sum()
   p=pn
```

Implement:
```
def _spectral_init(n, rng):
   m=2*n-1; k=np.arange(m); t=k/(m-1)
   candidates=[]
   for flat in [0.0,0.5,0.8]:
      shape=1.0 - flat*(2*t-1)**2
      shape=np.clip(shape,0,None)
      if shape.sum()>0:
         q=shape/shape.sum()
         p=_sqrt_from_conv(q,n)
         if p is not None: candidates.append(p)
   # random phases
   for _ in range(2):
      q=1 + 0.3*rng.normal(size=m); clip; normalize; p=...
   # smooth q with FFT? maybe not
   return normalize candidates
```
`_sqrt_from_conv`:
```
def _sqrt_from_conv(q,n, iters=6):
   L=_fft_len(2*n)  # need q length m roughly; use L≥2n? For P degree n-1, P P^? Actually q full length 2n-1; FFT length L=next_fast_len(2*n) enough to compute cyclic? Use L=next_fast_len(2*n) (power of 2).
   p=np.ones(n)/n
   for it in range(iters):
      P=rfft(p,L)
      Q=rfft(q,L)
      # avoid zeros
      denom=P*P; denom=np.where(np.abs(denom)>1e-300, denom, 1e-300)
      ratio=Q/denom
      # make ratio finite
      Pn=P*np.sqrt(ratio)
      np.real_if_close? Pn complex.
      pn=irfft(Pn,n=L)[:n]
      pn=np.nan_to_num(pn)
      pn=np.maximum(pn,0)
      s=pn.sum()
      if s<=0 or not finite: return None
      p=pn/s
   return p
```
This may converge to p with p*p close q. If q max low, p may have low max. Good. Add to inits for each length, maybe one candidate. But if p from sqrt has many zeros, LSE can refine.

### LSE function with L cache and valid conv inline
```
def _make_lse_fn(n, beta):
    m=2*n-1; L=_fft_len(m)
    scale = 4.0*n
    def f(y):
        y=np.asarray(y,dtype=np.float64)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)): return 1e100, np.zeros(n)
        z=y - y.max()
        z=np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
        e=np.exp(z); s=e.sum()
        if not finite or s<=0: ...
        p=e/s
        fa=rfft(p,L)
        q=irfft(fa*fa, n=L)[:m]
        q=np.maximum(q,0.0)
        mq=float(q.max())
        if not finite: ...
        zq=beta*(q-mq)
        zq=np.clip(zq, -50.0, 0.0)
        en=np.exp(zq); ss=en.sum()
        # ss should >0
        if ss<=0: w=np.zeros(m); w[int(np.argmax(q))]=1.0; log_s=-50/beta? But ss>=1.
        w=en/ss
        rap=rfft(p[::-1],L)
        rw=rfft(w,L)
        vc=irfft(rw*rap, n=L)[n-1:2*n-1]
        G=scale*vc
        gdot=float(np.dot(p,G))
        grad=p*(G-gdot)
        if not finite grad: grad=zeros; val=1e100
        else val=2.0*n*(mq + math.log(ss)/beta)
        return float(val), grad
    return f
```
Note `irfft(..., n=L)`? The parameter is `n` output length. In scipy, `irfft(zeros, n=L)` works if input length L//2+1. Use `irfft(arr, L)`? Signature `irfft(x, n=None, ...)`. To be safe, `irfft(fa*fa, L)`. The second positional is n. Yes. Use `irfft(fa * fa, L)`.
Potential if L is odd? next_fast_len can return odd. irfft requires n even? For real FFT length L, L can be any int? `rfft` length L requires L; `irfft` output length L, I think L should be even? FFTPACK: n must be even? Actually `scipy.fft.rfft` with n odd is allowed? For real FFT, output length n//2+1; inverse length n can be odd. It should work. next_fast_len returns integer with small prime factors, could be odd (e.g. 5). It's okay? `irfft` with odd n expects input length n//2+1. Good.

But if L odd, L//2+1 input length from rfft. okay.

In `_valid_conv` for LP? Not needed.

def _score_exact(a):
    try:
       fn=globals().get('evaluate_sequence')
       if fn is None: return inf
       arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).ravel()
       if arr.size==0: return inf
       arr=arr*((?) no) # sum1
       sc=fn(arr.tolist())
       return float(sc) if math.isfinite(sc) else inf
    except: return inf
```
`arr.tolist()` creates Python floats. Good.

### `_optimize_length` implementation
```
def _optimize_length(L, stop, rng, parent_seq):
    nonlocal best_seq,best_score
    if time.monotonic() >= stop-5: return
    # base init
    base = _interp_sequence(best_seq, L) if best_seq is not None else _interp_sequence(parent_seq, L)
    # inits list
    inits=[base]
    if parent_seq is not None and parent_seq.size != best_seq.size:
        inits.append(_interp_sequence(parent_seq, L))
    # spectral
    try:
        sp=_spectral_init(L, rng)
        inits.extend(sp)
    except: pass
    # random if enough
    if stop - time.monotonic() > 80:
        inits.append(_random_smooth(L, rng))
        if L<=1024 and stop - time.monotonic() > 120:
            inits.append(_random_smooth(L, rng))
    # dedup and score top
    scored=[]; seen=set()
    for a in inits:
       if time.monotonic() > stop-5: break
       a=_normalize(a)
       key=tuple(np.round(a,8).tolist()) maybe memory; skip? Use first 8 sum? We'll just score all, max 5.
       sc=_score_internal(a)
       if finite: scored.append((sc,a))
    if not scored: scored=[(_score_internal(base),base)]
    scored.sort(key=lambda x:x[0])
    # choose top 2 if stop large else 1
    n_top=2 if (stop - time.monotonic()) > 120 and L<=1024 else 1
    for idx,(sc0,a) in enumerate(scored[:n_top]):
       if time.monotonic() > stop-8: break
       p=a
       # dynamic stops within remaining
       t_start=time.monotonic()
       alloc=stop - t_start
       # coordinate 40%, LSE 30%, coordinate 20%, LP if L small within LSE phase
       c1_stop = min(stop-15, t_start + 0.35*alloc)
       p, _ = _coordinate_refine(p, c1_stop, seed=int(rng.integers(1,1_000_000)))
       _try_candidate(p)
       if time.monotonic() < stop-12:
          # LP for small before LSE? LP can be good. Do if L<=512 and remaining>40
          if L <= 512 and stop - time.monotonic() > 45:
              lp_stop = min(stop-12, time.monotonic() + min(60.0, 0.25*(stop-time.monotonic())))
              p2, _ = _lp_update(p, L, lp_stop, seed=int(rng.integers(1,1_000_000)))
              # continue from better of p,p2
              if _score_internal(p2) < _score_internal(p): p=p2
              _try_candidate(p)
          lse_stop = min(stop-8, time.monotonic() + max(15.0, 0.30*alloc))
          lambdas = _lse_lambdas(L, high=False)
          p, _ = _lse_refine(p, lse_stop, lambdas)
          _try_candidate(p)
       if time.monotonic() < stop-5:
          c2_stop = min(stop, time.monotonic() + max(5.0, 0.20*alloc))
          p, _ = _coordinate_refine(p, c2_stop, seed=int(rng.integers(1,1_000_000)))
          _try_candidate(p)
       # After primary, if still much time and idx==0 maybe random restart?
```
The nested time allocations can be tricky; ensure we don't exceed stop. The code may skip phases if too little.

Maybe better to have fixed sequence within length based on remaining continuously:
```
if stop - now > 20: coordinate until now + min(20? 0.3alloc)
if stop - now > 30: LSE until now + ...
if stop - now > 10: coordinate until stop-2
```
Current okay.

For L=2048, `alloc` large (~500s), LSE stop now+150s with maxfun table maybe less; coordinate c1 175s, c2 100s. LP skipped. Good.
For L=256, alloc maybe 100s, c1 35s, LSE 30s, c2 20s, LP maybe 25s. Good.

But if multiple inits, second will have less time. We might only top1 for large to save. Fine.

### `_spectral_init` may be too random and produce bad candidates; but scored and not chosen if bad. Limit to one candidate to save. Use iters=4.

```
def _spectral_init(n, rng):
    m=2*n-1; k=np.arange(m); t=k/(m-1)
    outs=[]
    for flat in (0.2,0.6):
       shape=1.0 - flat*(2*t-1)**2
       ...
    # one random smoothed q
    q=1+0.3*rng.normal(size=m); q=np.maximum(q,0.1); normalize maybe low-pass? We can smooth q by convolving with Gaussian? Use FFT? skip.
    # take only first candidate to not too much
    return outs
```
Maybe return list of up to 2.

`_sqrt_from_conv` with L=_fft_len(2*n) (not m) to avoid q length mismatch? rfft(q,L) zero pades length L. P length n. Q length m. Use L≥2n (power). Good.
Iteration:
```
P = rfft(p, L)
Q = rfft(q, L)
den = P*P
den = np.where(np.abs(den) > 1e-20, den, 1e-20)
ratio = Q/den
ratio = np.nan_to_num(ratio, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=-1e6)
Pn = P * np.sqrt(ratio)
pn = irfft(Pn, L)[:n]
pn = np.real(pn) # irfft real
pn = np.maximum(pn,0)
s=pn.sum()
if s<1e-12: break
p=pn/s
```
Finally return p.

### In coordinate, when updating p_new for away:
```
N = n-1
if N == 0: return
p_new = (1.0 - t) * p
p_new += (t / N)
p_new[i] -= t / N  # because want + t*(1/N) for all j except i, and 0 at i
```
Better:
```
p_new = (1-t)*p
p_new += t/N
p_new[i] -= t/N
```
This gives p_i=(1-t)p_i, others=(1-t)p_j+t/N. Sum =1-t +t =1. Good.
For vertex:
```
p_new=(1-t)*p; p_new[i]+=t
```
Then `p_new=p_new/p_new.sum()` to fix.

### Direct G active average: divide by len(active). For current p sum1, if active all, G ~ constant-ish. argmin/max random.

### Coordinate line search returns qnew possibly with tiny negative; we clip. But qnew sum may not be 1; for active q maybe okay. Use `qnew = np.maximum(qnew,0.0)`; no renormalize because max scale? If sum drift, future line search target formulas assume exact p? If q sum drifted due clip, small. We could normalize qnew by qnew.sum() to maintain sum1 (clipping negative changes sum). For max score, q scale matters? p sum1 => q sum1. Clip negatives could reduce sum small. Normalize qnew/=qnew.sum() to keep scale. But qnew from quadratic of nonnegative target and base should nonnegative except round; sum1. Do:
```
qs=qnew.sum(); if qs>0: qnew = qnew/qs
```
This could slightly alter max if base max? If sum <1 by 1e-15, okay. If qnew has zeros negative clipped? Sum nearly1. Do normalize.
In `_line_search_quad`, after qnew normalize, recompute `Mn=qnew.max()`. Good.

### `_line_search_quad` refine uses `vals2` from same base/b/c. If best t from coarse, refine. If refine best at 0, return None. Need exclude 0 in final: if `ib2 == 0` and refine includes 0, no improvement because maxs2[0]=currentM? But maybe if currentM not exactly due base max > maxs2[0] rounding? Use if `tt <= 0 or maxs2[ib2] >= currentM - tol: return None`.

### In `_coordinate_refine`, after accepted p update, M maybe from qnew max. We update `best` if M < bestM - tol. But p_new score maybe exact higher due q normalized? okay.

### LSE exact score after each beta uses `_score_internal`, which FFT. Good.

### Let's ensure `_optimize_length` top inits scoring doesn't take too long for spectral p. spectral p may be bad but score cost. okay.

### Selecting inits top 2: If `n_top=1` but top init is bad spectral and parent init second? We sort. Good.

Dedup not necessary; but if scored multiple same, waste. Could use simple `seen_keys` with rounding first 16 values? For L large key memory. We'll skip, but avoid same object: best and parent interp may same if same. If parent_seq size == best_seq.size and same array? Could add both. It's okay.

### If `_score_internal` of spectral init returns 2 or worse, sorted after. Good.

### More time-safe: `_optimize_length` after scoring inits, if `stop - now < 10` return. For each init, if `stop - now < 10` break.

### Let's now write final code. Ensure final is only code fence. The code can be long. I'll be careful with indentation and undefined variables.

One more issue: `irfft` may emit a RuntimeWarning if input non-Hermitian due to tiny imag; okay. Could use `np.ascontiguousarray`. no.

### Candidate exact final selection
```
candidates = [best_int_seq, best_ext_seq, parent_seq, fallback]
final_best = None; final_score = inf
for cand in candidates:
   if cand is None: continue
   arr=...
   sc=_score_exact(arr)
   if finite and sc < final_score:
       final_score=sc; final_best=arr
# if no finite, use internal best normalized
```
But `_score_exact` uses global evaluate_sequence. If it returns inf for large due to timeout? It won't. But if exact missing, use internal.
Return final_best.tolist(). Ensure finite/nonempty.

### If `best_int_seq` is None? initialize parent.

### Let's ensure no use of `height_sequence_1` literal. Good.

### Edge case: `budget_s` maybe array? convert float. If fail, 1300.

### We can include `_TIME`? no.

Let's proceed to final code.
</think>

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math

from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft, next_fast_len
from scipy.optimize import minimize, linprog

_FL_CACHE = {}
_KINFO_CACHE = {}

_T_COARSE = np.unique(np.concatenate(([0.0], np.geomspace(1e-5, 1.0, 44))))
_T_COARSE_SMALL = np.unique(np.concatenate(([0.0], np.geomspace(1e-5, 1.0, 30))))
_T_FINE = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, 49)


def _fft_len(m: int) -> int:
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 0:
        return 1
    v = _FL_CACHE.get(m)
    if v is None:
        v = int(next_fast_len(m))
        _FL_CACHE[m] = v
    return v


def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def _get_parent():
    try:
        h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    except Exception:
        h = None
    if h is None:
        return None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if p.size == 0:
        return None
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12:
        return None
    return p / s


def _full_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    L = _fft_len(m)
    fa = rfft(a, L)
    c = irfft(fa * fa, L)[:m]
    c = np.nan_to_num(c, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)


def _cross_conv(a, b):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n1, n2 = a.size, b.size
    if n1 == 0 or n2 == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    m = n1 + n2 - 1
    L = _fft_len(m)
    c = irfft(rfft(a, L) * rfft(b, L), L)[:m]
    c = np.nan_to_num(c, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)


def _score_internal(a):
    try:
        p = _normalize(a)
        n = int(p.size)
        if n <= 0:
            return math.inf
        q = _full_conv(p)
        if q.size == 0:
            return math.inf
        M = float(q.max())
        if not math.isfinite(M):
            return math.inf
        return float(2.0 * n * M)
    except Exception:
        return math.inf


def _score_exact(a):
    try:
        fn = globals().get("evaluate_sequence", None)
        if fn is None:
            return math.inf
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size == 0:
            return math.inf
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(arr.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
            return math.inf
        lst = [float(x) for x in arr]
        sc = float(fn(lst))
        if not math.isfinite(sc):
            return math.inf
        return sc
    except Exception:
        return math.inf


def _interp_sequence(a, L: int):
    L = int(L)
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if L <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
    if a.size == L:
        return _normalize(a)
    if a.size == 1:
        return np.full(L, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(a.size))
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, L)
    v = np.interp(x1, x0, a)
    return _normalize(v)


def _random_smooth(n: int, rng):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    xs = np.clip(x, 1e-8, 1.0 - 1e-8)
    f = None
    kind = int(rng.integers(0, 6))

    if kind == 0:
        a = float(rng.uniform(-3.0, 3.0))
        b = float(rng.uniform(-3.0, 3.0))
        f = np.exp(a * x + b * (1.0 - x) + float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, n)))
    elif kind == 1:
        aa = float(rng.uniform(0.2, 7.0))
        bb = float(rng.uniform(0.2, 7.0))
        f = np.power(xs, aa - 1.0) * np.power(1.0 - xs, bb - 1.0)
    elif kind == 2:
        f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for _ in range(int(rng.integers(1, 4))):
            c = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
            w = float(rng.uniform(0.03, 0.25))
            amp = float(rng.uniform(0.4, 2.0))
            f += amp * np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - c) / w) ** 2)
    elif kind == 3:
        a = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 4.0))
        b = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 4.0))
        f = np.power(xs, a) + 1.3 * np.power(1.0 - xs, b)
    else:
        f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for _ in range(int(rng.integers(1, 5))):
            m = int(rng.integers(1, 9))
            amp = float(rng.uniform(0.04, 0.55))
            ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
            f += amp * np.cos(math.pi * m * x + ph)

    if f is None:
        f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    f = np.nan_to_num(f, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0) + 1e-12
    return _normalize(f)


def _sqrt_from_conv(q_target, n: int, iters: int = 5):
    q_target = np.asarray(q_target, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(n)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    if q_target.size < m:
        q_target = np.resize(q_target, m)
    else:
        q_target = q_target[:m]
    s = float(q_target.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-14:
        return None
    q_target = q_target / s

    L = _fft_len(2 * n)
    p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    for _ in range(int(max(1, iters))):
        P = rfft(p, L)
        Q = rfft(q_target, L)
        den = P * P
        den = np.where(np.abs(den) > 1e-25, den, 1e-25 + 0j)
        ratio = Q / den
        ratio = np.nan_to_num(ratio, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=-1e6)
        Pn = P * np.sqrt(ratio)
        pn = irfft(Pn, L)[:n]
        pn = np.nan_to_num(pn, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        pn = np.maximum(pn, 0.0)
        ss = float(pn.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(ss) or ss < 1e-14:
            break
        p = pn / ss
    ss = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(ss) or ss <= 1e-14:
        return None
    return p / ss


def _spectral_inits(n: int, rng):
    out = []
    n = int(n)
    if n < 8:
        return out
    m = 2 * n - 1
    k = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
    t = k / max(1.0, float(m - 1))

    shapes = [
        1.0,
        1.0 - 0.45 * (2.0 * t - 1.0) ** 2,
        1.0 - 0.75 * (2.0 * t - 1.0) ** 2,
    ]
    for shape in shapes:
        q = np.maximum(shape, 0.0) + 1e-12
        q = q / max(1e-300, float(q.sum()))
        p = _sqrt_from_conv(q, n, iters=5)
        if p is not None:
            sc = _score_internal(p)
            if math.isfinite(sc):
                out.append(p)

    q = 1.0 + 0.25 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=m)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.05)
    q = q / max(1e-300, float(q.sum()))
    p = _sqrt_from_conv(q, n, iters=4)
    if p is not None:
        out.append(p)

    # Keep only the few best to avoid wasting budget.
    if len(out) > 2:
        scored = []
        for p in out:
            sc = _score_internal(p)
            if math.isfinite(sc):
                scored.append((sc, p))
        scored.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])
        out = [z[1] for z in scored[:2]]
    return out


def _p_from_y(y):
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if y.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    z = y - float(np.max(y))
    z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(y.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(y.size)
    return e / s


def _y_from_p(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if p.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-300)
    y = np.log(p)
    y = y - float(np.max(y))
    return np.nan_to_num(y, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)


def _make_lse_fn(n: int, beta: float):
    n = int(n)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    L = _fft_len(m)
    scale = 4.0 * float(n)

    def f(y):
        y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if y.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
            return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

        p = _p_from_y(y)
        if p.size != n:
            return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

        fa = rfft(p, L)
        q = irfft(fa * fa, L)[:m]
        q = np.nan_to_num(q, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
        mq = float(q.max())
        if not math.isfinite(mq) or mq <= 0.0:
            return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

        z = beta * (q - mq)
        z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
        e = np.exp(z)
        ss = float(e.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(ss) or ss <= 0.0:
            w = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
            w[int(np.argmax(q))] = 1.0
            log_s = 0.0
        else:
            w = e / ss
            log_s = math.log(ss)

        rap = rfft(p[::-1], L)
        rw = rfft(w, L)
        vc = irfft(rw * rap, L)[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
        vc = np.nan_to_num(vc, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        G = scale * vc

        gdot = float(np.dot(p, G))
        grad = p * (G - gdot)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
            grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            val = 1e100
        else:
            val = float(2.0 * n * (mq + log_s / beta))
            if not math.isfinite(val):
                val = 1e100
                grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        return val, grad

    return f


def _lse_lambdas(n: int, high: bool = False):
    n = int(n)
    if high:
        if n <= 256:
            return (300.0, 800.0, 2000.0)
        if n <= 512:
            return (250.0, 700.0)
        if n <= 1024:
            return (200.0, 600.0)
        return (150.0, 450.0)
    if n <= 256:
        return (10.0, 45.0, 180.0, 550.0)
    if n <= 512:
        return (10.0, 45.0, 180.0, 450.0)
    if n <= 1024:
        return (10.0, 35.0, 140.0, 380.0)
    if n <= 2048:
        return (10.0, 35.0, 140.0)
    return (10.0, 30.0)


def _maxfun_for_lse(n: int, rem: float):
    rem = max(0.0, float(rem))
    if n <= 256:
        cap, est = 700, 0.002
    elif n <= 512:
        cap, est = 480, 0.005
    elif n <= 1024:
        cap, est = 320, 0.012
    elif n <= 2048:
        cap, est = 220, 0.03
    else:
        cap, est = 140, 0.08
    mf = int(rem * 0.55 / max(1e-6, est))
    return max(30, min(cap, mf))


def _lse_refine(a0, stop: float, lambdas=None):
    p0 = _normalize(a0)
    n = int(p0.size)
    if n <= 1 or time.monotonic() > stop:
        return p0, _score_internal(p0)

    best = p0.copy()
    best_sc = _score_internal(best)
    current = p0.copy()
    current_sc = best_sc
    lam_list = list(lambdas) if lambdas is not None else _lse_lambdas(n)

    for lam in lam_list:
        if time.monotonic() > stop - 2.0:
            break
        q = _full_conv(current)
        qmax = float(q.max()) if q.size else 0.0
        if not math.isfinite(qmax) or qmax <= 1e-300:
            qmax = 1e-6
        beta = float(lam) / qmax
        if not math.isfinite(beta) or beta < 1.0:
            beta = 1.0
        beta = min(beta, 1e7)

        mf = _maxfun_for_lse(n, stop - time.monotonic())
        if mf < 30:
            break

        fn = _make_lse_fn(n, beta)
        y0 = _y_from_p(current)
        try:
            res = minimize(
                fn,
                y0,
                jac=True,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                options={
                    "maxiter": max(40, min(400, mf // 2)),
                    "maxfun": mf,
                    "ftol": 1e-12,
                    "gtol": 1e-9,
                    "maxls": 20,
                },
            )
        except Exception:
            break

        if not hasattr(res, "x") or res.x is None:
            break
        p_new = _p_from_y(res.x)
        sc_new = _score_internal(p_new)
        if not math.isfinite(sc_new):
            break

        if sc_new < best_sc - 1e-14:
            best = p_new.copy()
            best_sc = sc_new
            current = best
            current_sc = sc_new
        else:
            # Follow the smoothed surrogate a bit if it has not exploded.
            if getattr(res, "success", False) and sc_new < best_sc + 0.02:
                current = p_new
                current_sc = sc_new

    if best_sc <= current_sc:
        return best, best_sc
    return best, best_sc


def _kinfo(n: int):
    v = _KINFO_CACHE.get(n)
    if v is not None:
        return v
    m = 2 * n - 1
    k = np.arange(m, dtype=np.int64)
    C = np.minimum(k + 1, 2 * n - 1 - k).astype(np.float64)
    low = np.maximum(0, k - (n - 1)).astype(np.int64)
    high = np.minimum(n - 1, k).astype(np.int64)
    v = (k, C, low, high)
    _KINFO_CACHE[n] = v
    return v


def _line_search_quad(base, b, c, currentM):
    base = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if base.size == 0 or b.size != base.size or c.size != base.size:
        return None
    if not math.isfinite(currentM) or currentM <= 0.0:
        return None

    T = _T_COARSE if base.size <= 6000 else _T_COARSE_SMALL
    t = T[:, None]
    vals = base[None, :] + t * b[None, :] + (t * t) * c[None, :]
    vals = np.maximum(vals, 0.0)
    maxs = vals.max(axis=1)
    maxs = np.nan_to_num(maxs, nan=1e30, posinf=1e30, neginf=1e30)

    ib = int(np.argmin(maxs))
    tol = max(1e-14, 1e-13 * currentM)
    if ib == 0 or maxs[ib] >= currentM - tol:
        return None

    lo = float(T[ib - 1]) if ib > 0 else 0.0
    hi = float(T[ib]) if ib < len(T) - 1 else 1.0
    if hi <= lo:
        hi = min(1.0, lo + max(0.01, 0.25 * max(lo, 1e-4)))
    if hi > 1.0:
        hi = 1.0
    t2 = _T_FINE[(lo * 48):][:48] if False else np.linspace(lo, hi, 49)
    t2c = (t2 - lo)
    # reuse valuation directly
    vals2 = base[None, :] + t2[:, None] * b[None, :] + (t2[:, None] * t2[:, None]) * c[None, :]
    vals2 = np.maximum(vals2, 0.0)
    maxs2 = vals2.max(axis=1)
    maxs2 = np.nan_to_num(maxs2, nan=1e30, posinf=1e30, neginf=1e30)
    jb = int(np.argmin(maxs2))
    if t2[jb] <= 1e-15 or maxs2[jb] >= currentM - tol:
        return None

    tt = float(t2[jb])
    qnew = base + tt * b + (tt * tt) * c
    qnew = np.nan_to_num(qnew, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    qnew = np.maximum(qnew, 0.0)
    ss = float(qnew.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(ss) or ss <= 1e-14:
        return None
    qnew = qnew / ss
    Mnew = float(qnew.max())
    if not math.isfinite(Mnew):
        return None
    return tt, Mnew, qnew


def _line_search_vertex(q, p, i: int, n: int, kinfo):
    m = 2 * n - 1
    qpv = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    qpv[i:i + n] = p
    base = q
    b = 2.0 * (qpv - base)
    c = base - 2.0 * qpv
    c[2 * i] += 1.0
    return _line_search_quad(base, b, c, float(q.max()))


def _line_search_away(q, p, i: int, n: int, kinfo):
    if n <= 1:
        return None
    m = 2 * n - 1
    N = n - 1
    N2 = float(N * N)
    k, C, low, high = kinfo

    qv = C.copy()
    qv[i:i + n] -= 2.0
    qv[2 * i] += 1.0
    qv *= (1.0 / N2)

    cum = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cum[0] = 0.0
    if n:
        cum[1:] = np.cumsum(p)

    win = cum[high + 1] - cum[low]
    exclude = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    exclude[i:i + n] = p
    qpv = (win - exclude) / float(N)

    base = q
    b = 2.0 * (qpv - base)
    c = base - 2.0 * qpv + qv
    return _line_search_quad(base, b, c, float(base.max()))


def _coordinate_refine(a0, stop: float, seed=0, maxmoves=None):
    p = _normalize(a0)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n <= 1:
        return p, _score_internal(p)
    if time.monotonic() >= stop:
        return p, _score_internal(p)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    q = _full_conv(p)
    M = float(q.max())
    best = p.copy()
    bestM = M
    bestS = float(2.0 * n * M)

    if maxmoves is None:
        if n <= 256:
            maxmoves = 9000
        elif n <= 512:
            maxmoves = 5000
        elif n <= 1024:
            maxmoves = 2500
        else:
            maxmoves = 1200

    kinfo = _kinfo(n)
    no_imp = 0
    perturbs = 0
    K = max(3, min(32, max(1, n // 64)))
   moves = 0

    while time.monotonic() < stop - 0.2 and moves < int(maxmoves):
        moves += 1

        if moves % 24 == 0:
            q = _full_conv(p)
            M = float(q.max())
            if not math.isfinite(M):
                break

        m = 2 * n - 1
        if m <= K:
            active = np.arange(m, dtype=np.int64)
        else:
            active = np.argpartition(q, -(K))[-K:]
            active = active[np.argsort(q[active])[::-1]] if False else active.copy()

        G = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for kk in active:
            kk = int(kk)
            lo = kk - (n - 1)
            if lo < 0:
                lo = 0
            hi = kk
            if hi > n - 1:
                hi = n - 1
            if lo <= hi:
                G[lo:hi + 1] += p[kk - hi:kk - lo + 1][::-1]
        G /= max(1.0, float(active.size))

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(G)):
            G = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)

        high_i = int(np.argmax(G))
        low_i = int(np.argmin(G))
        r1 = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        r2 = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        cands = [(high_i, "away"), (low_i, "toward"), (r1, "away"), (r2, "toward")]

        improved = False
        for idx, direction in cands:
            if time.monotonic() > stop - 0.2:
                break
            idx = int(np.clip(idx, 0, n - 1))
            if direction == "away":
                res = _line_search_away(q, p, idx, n, kinfo)
            else:
                res = _line_search_vertex(q, p, idx, n, kinfo)

            if res is None:
                continue
            t, Mnew, qnew = res
            if t <= 0.0 or Mnew >= M - 1e-13:
                continue

            if direction == "away":
                N = float(n - 1)
                pnew = (1.0 - t) * p
                pnew += t / N
                pnew[idx] -= t / N
            else:
                pnew = (1.0 - t) * p
                pnew[idx] += t

            pnew = np.nan_to_num(pnew, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
            pnew = np.maximum(pnew, 0.0)
            s = float(pnew.sum())
            if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-14:
                continue
            pnew /= s

            p = pnew
            q = qnew
            M = Mnew
            if M < bestM - 1e-13:
                bestM = M
                best = p.copy()
                bestS = float(2.0 * n * M)
            improved = True
            break

        if improved:
            no_imp = 0
        else:
            no_imp += 1
            if no_imp >= 7 and perturbs < 30:
                scale = 0.025 * (1.0 / n) * (1.0 + 0.08 * min(10.0, float(no_imp)))
                p = p + rng.normal(0.0, scale, n)
                p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
                s = float(p.sum())
                if s > 1e-14 and math.isfinite(s):
                    p /= s
                    q = _full_conv(p)
                    M = float(q.max())
                    if math.isfinite(M) and M < bestM:
                        bestM = M
                        best = p.copy()
                        bestS = float(2.0 * n * M)
                no_imp = 0
                perturbs += 1
            if no_imp >= 120 or perturbs >= 40:
                break

    if bestM < M:
        p = best.copy()
    return p, bestS


def _lp_update(f0, n: int, stop: float, seed=0):
    n = int(n)
    f = _normalize(f0)
    if n < 4 or time.monotonic() >= stop:
        return f, _score_internal(f)

    best = f.copy()
    best_sc = _score_internal(best)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    obj = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    obj[-1] = 1.0
    b_eq = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    max_iter = 14 if n <= 256 else 8
    no_imp = 0
    m = 2 * n - 1

    for _ in range(max_iter):
        if time.monotonic() > stop - 6.0:
            break

        A = np.zeros((m, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(n):
            A[j:j + n, j] = f[j]
        A[:, -1] = -1.0
        b_ub = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
        A_eq = np.zeros((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
        A_eq[0, :n] = 1.0
        bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n + [(0.0, None)]

        try:
            res = linprog(
                obj,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b_ub,
                A_eq=A_eq,
                b_eq=b_eq,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
                options={"presolve": True},
            )
        except Exception:
            break

        if res is None or getattr(res, "status", 1) != 0 or getattr(res, "x", None) is None:
            break

        g = np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=np.float64)
        g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        g = np.clip(g, 0.0, 1.0)
        gs = float(g.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(gs) or gs < 1e-12:
            g = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        else:
            g /= gs

        cf = _full_conv(f)
        cfs = _cross_conv(f, g)
        cg = _full_conv(g)
        bcoef = 2.0 * (cfs - cf)
        ccoef = cf - 2.0 * cfs + cg
        ls = _line_search_quad(cf, bcoef, ccoef, float(cf.max()))
        if ls is None:
            no_imp += 1
            if no_imp >= 4:
                f = f + rng.normal(0.0, 0.03 / n, n)
                f = np.clip(f, 0.0, None)
                fs = f.sum()
                if fs > 1e-12:
                    f /= fs
                no_imp = 0
                if _score_internal(f) < best_sc:
                    best = f.copy()
                    best_sc = _score_internal(best)
            continue

        t, Mnew, qnew = ls
        pnew = (1.0 - t) * f + t * g
        pnew = np.nan_to_num(pnew, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        pnew = np.clip(pnew, 0.0, None)
        s = float(pnew.sum())
        if s <= 1e-12 or not math.isfinite(s):
            continue
        pnew /= s

        sc = _score_internal(pnew)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
            best = pnew.copy()
            best_sc = sc
            f = pnew
            q = qnew
            no_imp = 0
        else:
            no_imp += 1

    return best, best_sc


def _optimize_length(L: int, stop: float, rng, parent_seq, best_int_seq, try_candidate):
    L = int(L)
    if L <= 1 or time.monotonic() > stop - 8:
        return

    t_start = time.monotonic()
    alloc = max(1.0, stop - t_start)

    inits = []
    if best_int_seq is not None:
        inits.append(_interp_sequence(best_int_seq, L))
    if parent_seq is not None:
        inits.append(_interp_sequence(parent_seq, L))
    else:
        inits.append(np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L))

    if stop - time.monotonic() > 35:
        try:
            inits.extend(_spectral_inits(L, rng))
        except Exception:
            pass

    if stop - time.monotonic() > 90:
        inits.append(_random_smooth(L, rng))
        if L <= 1024 and stop - time.monotonic() > 140:
            inits.append(_random_smooth(L, rng))

    scored = []
    seen = set()
    for a in inits:
        if stop - time.monotonic() < 6:
            break
        a = _normalize(a)
        if a.size != L:
            continue
        key = tuple(np.round(a[:min(32, a.size)] + (a.sum() if a.size > 32 else 0.0), 8).tolist())
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        sc = _score_internal(a)
        if math.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, a))
    if not scored:
        base = _normalize(inits[0] if inits else np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64))
        scored = [(_score_internal(base), base)]

    scored.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])
    n_top = 1
    if stop - time.monotonic() > 160 and L <= 1024:
        n_top = 2

    for _, a in scored[:n_top]:
        if time.monotonic() > stop - 12:
            break
        p = a

        c1_stop = min(stop - 18, t_start + 0.34 * alloc)
        if c1_stop - time.monotonic() > 8:
            p, _ = _coordinate_refine(
                p,
                c1_stop,
                seed=int(rng.integers(1, 10_000_000)),
            )
            try_candidate(p)

        if time.monotonic() < stop - 14:
            if L <= 512 and stop - time.monotonic() > 45:
                lp_stop = min(stop - 14, time.monotonic() + max(12.0, 0.18 * alloc))
                p2, _ = _lp_update(p, L, lp_stop, seed=int(rng.integers(1, 10_000_000)))
                try_candidate(p2)
                if _score_internal(p2) < _score_internal(p):
                    p = p2

            lse_stop = min(stop - 10, time.monotonic() + max(14.0, 0.33 * alloc))
            if lse_stop - time.monotonic() > 10:
                p, _ = _lse_refine(
                    p,
                    lse_stop,
                    lambdas=_lse_lambdas(L, high=False),
                )
                try_candidate(p)

        if time.monotonic() < stop - 5:
            c2_stop = min(stop - 2, time.monotonic() + max(6.0, 0.20 * alloc))
            p, _ = _coordinate_refine(
                p,
                c2_stop,
                seed=int(rng.integers(1, 10_000_000)),
            )
            try_candidate(p)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = max(10.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        budget = 1300.0
    hard_deadline = t0 + budget
    reserve = max(12.0, min(70.0, 0.05 * (hard_deadline - t0)))
    deadline = hard_deadline - reserve

    try:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    except Exception:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(42)

    parent = _get_parent()
    if parent is None:
        parent = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
    else:
        parent = _normalize(parent)

    fallback = parent.copy()
    best_int_seq = parent.copy()
    best_int_score = _score_internal(best_int_seq)
    if not math.isfinite(best_int_score):
        best_int_score = 1e30
    best_exact_seq = parent.copy()
    best_exact_score = _score_exact(parent)
    if not math.isfinite(best_exact_score):
        best_exact_score = best_int_score

    def try_candidate(arr):
        nonlocal best_int_seq, best_int_score, best_exact_seq, best_exact_score
        if arr is None:
            return
        p = _normalize(arr)
        if p.size == 0:
            return
        sc = _score_internal(p)
        if not math.isfinite(sc):
            return
        if sc < best_int_score - 1e-10:
            best_int_score = sc
            best_int_seq = p.copy()
        if p.size <= 1600 or sc < best_int_score + 1e-8:
            esc = _score_exact(p)
            if math.isfinite(esc) and esc < best_exact_score - 1e-9:
                best_exact_score = esc
                best_exact_seq = p.copy()

    # Very short budget: quick local polish and return.
    if budget < 70.0 or deadline <= t0 + 12.0:
        stop = max(t0 + 5.0, deadline - 3.0)
        try:
            p, _ = _coordinate_refine(best_int_seq, stop, seed=123)
            try_candidate(p)
        except Exception:
            pass
        return [float(x) for x in _normalize(best_int_seq)]

    # Choose lengths to explore.
    parent_n = int(parent.size)
    lens = [256, 512, 1024, 2048]
    if parent_n not in lens:
        lens.append(parent_n)
    lens = sorted(set(int(x) for x in lens if int(x) >= 16))

    if budget < 180.0:
        lens = [L for L in lens if L <= 512]
    elif budget < 450.0:
        lens = [L for L in lens if L <= 1024]
    if not lens:
        lens = [max(16, parent_n)]

    # Allocate the main search budget.
    final_reserve = max(8.0, min(70.0, 0.06 * (deadline - t0)))
    main_end = max(t0 + 20.0, deadline - final_reserve)
    total_main = max(1.0, main_end - t0)

    # cumulative fractions
    known_frac = {256: 0.10, 512: 0.28, 1024: 0.60, 2048: 0.92}
    fracs = []
    for i, L in enumerate(lens):
        if L in known_frac:
            fracs.append(known_frac[L])
        else:
            fracs.append((i + 1) / max(1, len(lens)))
    fracs = sorted(set(fracs))

    stops = []
    prev = t0
    for L, fr in zip(lens, [None] + fracs[:-1]):
        next_stop = t0 + (fr if fr is not None else 1.0) * total_main
        next_stop = min(next_stop, main_end)
        stops.append(max(prev + 1.0, next_stop))
        prev = max(prev, next_stop)
    if len(lens) > 0:
        stops[-1] = main_end

    try:
        for L, stop in zip(lens, stops):
            if stop <= time.monotonic() + 12.0:
                continue
            _optimize_length(
                L,
                float(stop),
                rng,
                parent,
                best_int_seq,
                try_candidate,
            )
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Final global polish in the reserved-but-safe zone.
    final_stop = max(time.monotonic(), min(deadline - 4.0, hard_deadline - 8.0))
    if best_int_seq is not None and final_stop - time.monotonic() > 12.0:
        n = int(best_int_seq.size)
        try:
            p, _ = _coordinate_refine(
                best_int_seq,
                min(final_stop - 10.0, time.monotonic() + max(10.0, 0.35 * (final_stop - time.monotonic()))),
                seed=987654321,
            )
            try_candidate(p)
        except Exception:
            pass

        if final_stop - time.monotonic() > 25.0 and n <= 2048:
            try:
                p, _ = _lse_refine(
                    best_int_seq,
                    final_stop - 8.0,
                    lambdas=_lse_lambdas(n, high=True),
                )
                try_candidate(p)
            except Exception:
                pass

        if final_stop - time.monotonic() > 8.0:
            try:
                p, _ = _coordinate_refine(best_int_seq, final_stop - 4.0, seed=13579)
                try_candidate(p)
            except Exception:
                pass

    # Pick the best by exact evaluator among tracked candidates.
    candidates = [best_int_seq, best_exact_seq, parent, fallback]
    final_seq = None
    final_score = math.inf
    seen_cand = set()

    cand_seq = sorted(lens)[-1] if lens else best_int_seq.size
    # Optionally include the longest length version of best_int_seq.
    try:
        cand_long = _interp_sequence(best_int_seq, max(best_int_seq.size, cand_seq))
        candidates.append(cand_long)
    except Exception:
        pass

    for cand in candidates:
        if cand is None:
            continue
        arr = np.nan_to_num(np.asarray(cand, dtype=np.float64).ravel(), nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(arr.sum())
        if arr.size == 0 or not math.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-8:
            continue
        key = (arr.size, tuple(np.round(arr[:16], 10).tolist()))
        if key in seen_cand:
            continue
        seen_cand.add(key)
        esc = _score_exact(arr)
        if math.isfinite(esc) and esc < final_score:
            final_score = esc
            final_seq = arr

    if final_seq is None:
        arr = np.nan_to_num(np.asarray(best_int_seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel(), nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(arr.sum())
        if arr.size == 0 or not math.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-8:
            arr = np.ones(32, dtype=np.float64) / 32.0
        else:
            arr = arr / s
        final_seq = arr

    final_seq = np.clip(final_seq, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if final_seq.size == 0:
        final_seq = np.ones(32, dtype=np.float64) / 32.0
    # Make sure the evaluator definitely accepts scale and cap.
    final_seq = final_seq / max(1e-12, float(final_seq.sum()))
    return [float(x) for x in final_seq]
```
